SDA> EXAMINE/INSTRUCTION FFFFFFFF.801151C0:FFFFFFFF.801151E0
IOC_STD$CREATE_DIOBM_C+001D0:   LDQ             R16,#X0010(R27)
IOC_STD$CREATE_DIOBM_C+001D4:   BIS             R31,#X02,R20
IOC_STD$CREATE_DIOBM_C+001D8:   BIS             R31,#X01,R0
IOC_STD$CREATE_DIOBM_C+001DC:   INSBL           R20,R17,R20
IOC_STD$CREATE_DIOBM_C+001E0:   LDL             R16,(R16)
IOC_STD$CREATE_DIOBM_C+001E4:   AND             R16,#X04,R16
IOC_STD$CREATE_DIOBM_C+001E8:   BNE             R16,#X000005
IOC_STD$CREATE_DIOBM_C+001EC:   LDQ_U           R19,(R17)
IOC_STD$CREATE_DIOBM_C+001F0:   BIC             R19,R20,R19
IOC_STD$CREATE_DIOBM_C+001F4:   STQ_U           R19,(R17)

The callee's first instruction is LDQ R16,#X0010(R27) — it reads its own procedure descriptor through R27, which the caller set at +641AC via BIS R31,R0,R27. Descriptor+0x10 is FFFFFFFF.8180A708, and the callee immediately does LDL R16,(R16) / AND R16,#X04 on it — a global flag test through a pointer stored in the descriptor. Caller and callee agree on the calling convention and on the descriptor contents. This is a real entry point; SDA is labeling by nearest global symbol, so IOC_STD$CREATE_DIOBM_C+001D0 is almost certainly a local routine following that global, not a mid-routine branch.

Section 6 of the task doc is dead. Nothing upstream corrupted anything.

But H1 is broader than "dropped writeback," and the doc under-specifies it. Three mechanisms produce a wrong R26 with correct memory:

Writeback dropped. The load read correctly and never committed; R26 retained a stale value.
Translation wrong. Correct VA, correct displacement, but EmulatR's DTB mapped it to the wrong PA and returned whatever lives there. This is exactly the wrong-but-valid PTE case — resolves silently, logs nothing, counts as "resolved" in your fault log, and survives PE-2/PE-3 untouched.
Data path corruption between the memory read and the register file.

The dump cannot distinguish these. SDA maps through the saved page tables, so it shows what the correct translation yields regardless of what EmulatR's DTB was doing at the time.

Widen or repeat across ranges as needed — worth checking S0/S1 and the nonpaged pool region at minimum. A hit gives you the PA that EmulatR's translation landed on instead of 818DF200, which is a direct read on the TLB defect. No hits across a broad sweep pushes hard toward mechanism 1.

Two things already ruled out, worth recording so section 5 doesn't chase them. R0 was correct at +641AC and nothing writes R0 between +64164 and +64194, so a stale base register would require register-file read inconsistency — exotic. And no displacement within the descriptor yields the bad value (+0 is 0000020000003008, +0x10 is 8180A708, +0x18 is 81808B48), so an offset bug on the correct base is out.
